For instance, workers from [Georgia](48768.md) are subject to heightened scrutiny when applying for entry visas, and an outright ban was imposed on the entry of domestic workers from [Guinea-Bissau](12186.md) and [Vietnam](202354.md).[^cite_note-337] Workers from [Bangladesh](3454.md) are also banned.[^cite_note-338] In April 2019, Kuwait added Ethiopia, Burkina Faso, Bhutan, Guinea and Guinea-Bissau to the list of banned countries bringing the total to 20. According to Migrant Rights, the bans are put in place mainly due to the fact that these countries lack embassies and labour corporations in Kuwait.[^cite_note-339]

### Petroleum and natural gas

Main article: [Petroleum industry in Kuwait](15554971.md)

Despite its relatively small territory, Kuwait has proven crude [oil reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_proven_oil_reserves#Countries) of 104 billion barrels, estimated to be 10% of the world's reserves. Kuwait also has substantial [natural gas reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_natural_gas_proven_reserves). All natural resources in the country are state property.

As part of Kuwait Vision 2035, Kuwait aims to position itself as a global hub for the petrochemical industry.[^cite_note-kuwaitvision-340] [Al Zour Refinery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Zour_Refinery) is the largest refinery in the Middle East.[^cite_note-zour1-341][^cite_note-zour2-342][^cite_note-zour3-343] It is Kuwait's largest environmentally friendly oil refinery,[^cite_note-alzour_env-344][^cite_note-kuwaitvision-340] where this refers to the effect on the local environment as opposed to the global environmental impact of burning the resulting oil. This Al Zour Refinery is a Kuwait-China cooperation project under the [Belt and Road Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belt_and_Road_Initiative).[^cite_note-chinakuwaitzour-345] Al Zour LNG Terminal is the Middle East's largest import terminal for [liquefied natural gas](832128.md).[^cite_note-lng-346][^cite_note-lng1-347][^cite_note-lng2-348] It is the world's largest capacity LNG storage and regasification green field project.[^cite_note-lng7-349][^cite_note-lng8-350] The project has attracted investments worth US\$3 billion.[^cite_note-lng4-351][^cite_note-lng3-352] Other megaprojects include biofuel and clean fuels.[^cite_note-argus-353][^cite_note-biofuel-354] On January 20, 2025, the [Kuwait Oil Company](1437937.md) announced a major hydrocarbon discovery at the Al Jlaiaa offshore field, which it sees as a significant milestone in the nation's energy sector. The development aligns with Kuwait's strategic plans to enhance its offshore exploration and production capabilities.[^cite_note-355][^cite_note-356]

### Health

Further information: [Health in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Kuwait) and [Healthcare in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Healthcare_in_Kuwait)



[Amiri Hospital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amiri_Hospital) in Kuwait

Kuwait has a state-funded healthcare system, which provides treatment without charge to Kuwaiti nationals. There are outpatient clinics in every residential area in Kuwait. A public insurance scheme exists to provide reduced cost healthcare to expatriates. Private healthcare providers also run medical facilities in the country, available to members of their insurance schemes. As part of Kuwait Vision 2035, many new hospitals recently opened.[^cite_note-health4-357][^cite_note-health5-358][^cite_note-health1-359] In the years leading up to the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), Kuwait invested in its health care system at a rate that was proportionally higher than most other GCC countries.[^cite_note-health3-360] Under the Kuwait Vision 2035 healthcare strategy, the public hospital sector significantly increased its capacity. Many new hospitals recently opened, Kuwait currently has 20 public hospitals.[^cite_note-health6-361][^cite_note-health5-358][^cite_note-health4-357][^cite_note-health1-359] The new Sheikh Jaber Al-Ahmad Hospital is the largest hospital in the Middle East.[^cite_note-health2-362] Kuwait also has 16 private hospitals.[^cite_note-health4-357]

Private sector hospitals in Kuwait offer multiple specialties. This trend is likely to grow further, especially in tapping opportunities to reduce treatments performed overseas and develop inbound medical tourism market by developing high end specialty hospitals.[^cite_note-363]

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Kuwait)

Kuwait has a modern network of highways. [Roadways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roads_in_Kuwait) extended 5,749 km (3,572 mi), of which 4,887 km (3,037 mi) is paved. There are more than two million passenger cars, and 500,000 commercial taxis, buses, and trucks in use. On major highways the maximum speed is 120 km/h (75 mph). Since there is no railway system in the country, most people travel by automobiles.



[Kuwait International Airport](464414.md) - Terminal 4



A highway in Kuwait City

The country's public transportation network consists almost entirely of bus routes. The state owned Kuwait Public Transportation Company was established in 1962. It runs local bus routes across Kuwait as well as longer distance services to other Gulf states. The main private bus company is CityBus, which operates about 20 routes across the country. Another private bus company, Kuwait Gulf Link Public Transport Services, was started in 2006. It runs local bus routes across Kuwait and longer distance services to neighbouring countries.

The Kuwait Ports Public Authority manages and operates ports across Kuwait. The country's principal commercial seaports are [Shuwaikh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shuwaikh) and Shuaiba, which handled combined cargo of 753,334 TEU in 2006.[^cite_note-364] Mina Al-Ahmadi is the largest port in the country.

There are two airports in Kuwait. [Kuwait International Airport](464414.md) serves as the principal hub for international air travel. State-owned [Kuwait Airways](377382.md) is the largest airline in the country. A portion of the airport complex is designated as Al Mubarak Air Base, which contains the headquarters of the [Kuwait Air Force](4641117.md), as well as the Kuwait Air Force Museum. In 2004, the first private airline of Kuwait, [Jazeera Airways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazeera_Airways), was launched. In 2005, the second private airline, [Wataniya Airways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wataniya_Airways) was founded.

As of May 2025, Kuwait International Airport is facing serious problems, as 14 international airlines have stopped flying to the airport. This is due to issues such as management problems, a weak economy, and tough competition from nearby airports. Fewer flights have led to fewer passengers, causing worries about the airport's future and its dwindling importance in the region.[^cite_note-365]

### Education

Main article: [Education in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Kuwait)



[Kuwait University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_University)

The general education system consists of four levels: [kindergarten](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kindergarten) (lasting for 2 years), [primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_education) (lasting for 5 years), [intermediate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_school) (lasting for 4 years) and [secondary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secondary_education) (lasting for 3 years).[^cite_note-366] Schooling at primary and intermediate level is compulsory for all students aged 6 – 14. All the levels of state education, including higher education, are free.[^cite_note-367] The public education system is undergoing a revamp due to a project in conjunction with the [World Bank](36752.md).[^cite_note-oxfordbusinessgroup-368][^cite_note-369] There are two public universities and 14 private universities.

### Finance

The [Kuwait Investment Authority](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Investment_Authority) (KIA) is Kuwait's largest [sovereign wealth fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sovereign_wealth_fund) specializing in foreign investment. The KIA is the world's oldest sovereign wealth fund. Since 1953, the Kuwaiti government has directed investments into Europe, the United States and the [Asia–Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia%E2%80%93Pacific). In 2021, the holdings were valued at around \$700 billion in assets. It is the [3rd largest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sovereign_wealth_fund#Largest_sovereign_wealth_funds) sovereign wealth fund in the world.[^cite_note-bloomberg_swf-370][^cite_note-swf1-371]

Kuwait previously had a leading position in the financial industry in the GCC. The previous Emir had promoted the idea that Kuwait should focus its energies, in terms of economic development, on the financial industry. The historical preeminence of Kuwait (among the GCC monarchies) in finance dated back to the founding of the [National Bank of Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Kuwait) in 1952. The bank was the first local publicly traded corporation in the GCC region. In the late 1970s and early 1980s, an alternative stock market, trading in shares of GCC companies, emerged in Kuwait, the [Souk Al-Manakh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Souk_Al-Manakh_stock_market_crash). At its peak, its market capitalization was the third highest in the world, behind only the United States and Japan, and ahead of the United Kingdom and France.[^cite_note-wages-372]

Kuwait's Islamic banking sector expanded following the enactment of Law No. 33 of 2003, which established a dedicated regulatory framework for Islamic banking. [Boubyan Bank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boubyan_Bank) was subsequently established in 2004 as Kuwait's first Islamic bank under the new framework.[^cite_note-373]

Kuwait is a major source of foreign economic assistance to other states through the [Kuwait Fund for Arab Economic Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Fund_for_Arab_Economic_Development), an autonomous state institution created in 1961 on the pattern of international development agencies. In 1974, the fund's lending mandate was expanded to include all [developing countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developing_countries) in the world.



[Central Bank of Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Bank_of_Kuwait)

In the past five years, there has been a rise in [entrepreneurship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrepreneurship) and small business start-ups in Kuwait.[^cite_note-374][^cite_note-375] The [informal sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Informal_sector) is also on the rise,[^cite_note-376] mainly due to the popularity of Instagram businesses.[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378][^cite_note-379] In 2020, Kuwait ranked fourth in the MENA region in startup funding after the UAE, Egypt and Saudi Arabia.[^cite_note-crisis3-380]

### Other non-oil industries

The biggest non-oil industry is steel manufacturing.[^cite_note-steel4-381][^cite_note-steel1-382][^cite_note-steel2-383][^cite_note-steel3-384][^cite_note-steel-385] United Steel Industrial Company (KWT Steel) is Kuwait's main steel manufacturing company, which caters to all of Kuwait's domestic market demands (particularly construction). Kuwait is self-sufficient in steel.[^cite_note-steel1-382][^cite_note-steel4-381][^cite_note-steel2-383][^cite_note-steel3-384]

In 2016, Kuwait's food self-sufficiency ratio was 49.5% in vegetables, 38.7% in meat, 12.4% in dairy, 24.9% in fruits, and 0.4% in cereals.[^cite_note-food_selfsufficient-386] 8.5% of Kuwait's entire territory consists of agricultural land, although arable land constitutes 0.6% of Kuwait's entire territory.[^cite_note-agri2-387][^cite_note-agri3-388] Historically, Jahra was a predominantly agricultural area. There are currently various farms in Jahra.[^cite_note-389]

Kuwait was ranked 73rd in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-390][^cite_note-391] According to the [United States Patent and Trademark Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Patent_and_Trademark_Office), Kuwait registered 448 [patents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patents) as of 31 December 2015.[^cite_note-uspto-392][^cite_note-393][^cite_note-orient-394][^cite_note-395] In the early to mid 2010s, Kuwait produced the largest number of scientific publications and patents per capita in the region and registered the highest growth regionally.[^cite_note-gain-396][^cite_note-397][^cite_note-398][^cite_note-399][^cite_note-mit-400][^cite_note-orient-394]

Kuwait was the first country in the region to implement [5G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/5G) technology. Kuwait is among the world's leading markets in 5G penetration.[^cite_note-5g-401][^cite_note-5gkw-402]

Tourism in Kuwait still remains very limited due to poor infrastructure and the alcohol ban. The annual "Hala Febrayer" festival somewhat attracts tourists from neighboring GCC countries, and includes a variety of events including music concerts, parades, and carnivals.[^cite_note-halafeb-403][^cite_note-404][^cite_note-405] The festival is a month-long commemoration of the [liberation of Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_of_Kuwait), and runs from 1 to 28 February. [Liberation Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Day) itself is celebrated on 26 February.[^cite_note-406]



The Palms Beach Hotel & Spa in Kuwait

In 2020, Kuwait's domestic travel and tourism spending was \$6.1 billion. The [WTTC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Travel_and_Tourism_Council) named Kuwait as one of the world's fastest-growing countries in travel and tourism [GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GDP) in 2019, with 11.6% year-on-year growth.[^cite_note-inbound-407] In 2016, the tourism industry generated nearly \$500 million in revenue.[^cite_note-408] In 2015, tourism accounted for 1.5 percent of the GDP.[^cite_note-409][^cite_note-410] [Sabah Al Ahmad Sea City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabah_Al_Ahmad_Sea_City) is one of Kuwait's biggest attractions.

The [Amiri Diwan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amiri_Diwan_of_Kuwait) recently inaugurated the new [Kuwait National Cultural District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_National_Cultural_District) (KNCD), which comprises [Sheikh Abdullah Al Salem Cultural Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Abdullah_Al_Salem_Cultural_Centre), [Sheikh Jaber Al Ahmad Cultural Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Jaber_Al_Ahmad_Cultural_Centre), [Al Shaheed Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Shaheed_Park), and [Al Salam Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Salam_Palace_\(Kuwait\)).[^cite_note-kncd2-411][^cite_note-kncd-412][^cite_note-kncd3-413][^cite_note-newkuwait-414] With a capital cost of more than US\$1 billion, the project is one of the largest cultural investments in the world.[^cite_note-kncd-412] The Kuwait National Cultural District is a member of the [Global Cultural Districts Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Cultural_Districts_Network).[^cite_note-gcdn-415] Al Shaheed Park is the largest green roof project ever undertaken in the region.[^cite_note-zinco-416]

Space and satellite programmes

See also: [Kuwait Space Rocket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Space_Rocket)



Prototype of the [Kuwait Space Rocket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Space_Rocket)

Kuwait has an emerging space industry which is largely driven by private sector initiatives.[^cite_note-space-417] Seven years after the launch of the world's first communications satellite, [Telstar 1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telstar_1), Kuwait in October 1969 inaugurated the first satellite ground station in the Middle East, "Um Alaish".[^cite_note-418] The Um Alaish satellite station complex housed several satellite ground stations including Um Alaish 1 (1969), Um Alaish 2 (1977), and Um Alaish 3 (1981). It provided satellite communication services in Kuwait until 1990 when it was destroyed by the Iraqi armed forces during the Iraqi invasion of Kuwait.[^cite_note-419] In 2019, Kuwait's Orbital Space established an amateur satellite ground station to provide free access to signals from satellites in orbit passing over Kuwait. The station was named Um Alaish 4 to continue the legacy of "Um Alaish" satellite station.[^cite_note-420] Um Alaish 4 is a member of FUNcube distributed [ground station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ground_station) network[^cite_note-421] and the Satellite Networked Open Ground Station project ([SatNOGS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SatNOGS)).[^cite_note-422]

Kuwait's Orbital Space in collaboration with the Space Challenges Program[^cite_note-423] and [EnduroSat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EnduroSat)[^cite_note-424] introduced an international initiative called "Code in Space". The initiative allows students from around the world to send and execute their own code in space.[^cite_note-425] The code is transmitted from a satellite ground station to a [cubesat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CubeSat) ([nanosatellite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_satellite)) orbiting earth 500 km (310 mi) above sea level. The code is then executed by the satellite's onboard computer and tested under real space environment conditions. The nanosatellite is called "QMR-KWT" (Arabic: قمر الكويت) which means "Moon of Kuwait", translated from Arabic.[^cite_note-first_satellite2-426] QMR-KWT launched to space on 30 June 2021[^cite_note-first_satellite-427] on [SpaceX](832774.md) [Falcon 9 Block 5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Falcon_9_Block_5) rocket and was part of the payload of a satellite carrier called ION SCV Dauntless David by D-Orbit.[^cite_note-428] It was deployed into its final orbit ([Sun-synchronous orbit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sun-synchronous_orbit)) on 16 July 2021.[^cite_note-429] QMR-KWT is Kuwait's first satellite.[^cite_note-first_satellite-427][^cite_note-430][^cite_note-first_satellite2-426] Building on the achievements of QMR-KWT, Orbital Space launched QMR-KWT 2 as Kuwait's 3rd satellite on 28 December 2025 aboard a Soyuz-2.1b rocket from Vostochny Cosmodrome in Russia. Like its predecessor, QMR-KWT 2 mission emphasizes accessibility, allowing students and young engineers to interact with an operational spacecraft in orbit.[^cite_note-431][^cite_note-432][^cite_note-433]

The [Kuwait Space Rocket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Space_Rocket) (KSR) is a Kuwaiti project to build and launch the first [suborbital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suborbital_spaceflight) [liquid bi-propellant rocket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liquid-propellant_rocket) in [Arabia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabia).[^cite_note-434] The project is divided into two phases with two separate vehicles: an initial testing phase with KSR-1 as a [test vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Test_article_\(aerospace\)) capable of reaching an altitude of 8 km (5.0 mi) and a more expansive suborbital test phase with the KSR-2 planned to fly to an altitude of 100 km (62 mi).[^cite_note-435]

Kuwait's Orbital Space in collaboration with the [Kuwait Scientific Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Scientific_Center) (TSCK) introduced for the first time in Kuwait the opportunity for students to send a science experiment to space. The objectives of this initiative was to allow students to learn about (a) how science space missions are done; (b) [microgravity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Micro-g_environment) (weightlessness) environment; (c) how to do science like a real scientist. This opportunity was made possible through Orbital Space agreement with [DreamUp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DreamUp) PBC and [Nanoracks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NanoRacks) LLC, which are collaborating with [NASA](18426568.md) under a Space Act Agreement.[^cite_note-436] The students' experiment was named "Kuwait's Experiment: E.coli Consuming Carbon Dioxide to Combat Climate Change".[^cite_note-437][^cite_note-438] The experiment was launched on [SpaceX CRS-21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SpaceX_CRS-21) (SpX-21) spaceflight to the [International Space Station](15043.md) (ISS) on 6 December 2020. Astronaut [Shannon Walker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shannon_Walker) (member of the ISS [Expedition 64](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expedition_64)) conducted the experiment on behalf of the students. In July 2021, Kuwait University announced that it is launching a national satellite project as part of state-led efforts to pioneer the country's sustainable space sector.[^cite_note-national_satellite-439][^cite_note-national_satellite2-440] KuwaitSat-1 was launched on 3 Jaunray 2023.[^cite_note-441] In 2024, Kuwait Foundation for the Advancement of Sciences (KFAS) established the Kuwait National Space Research Center (KNSRC), providing a dedicated institutional platform for space research, policy, and international cooperation. KNSRC is located at Kuwait University.[^cite_note-442][^cite_note-443]

## Demographics

Main article: [Demographics of Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Kuwait)



Kuwaiti youth celebrating Kuwait's independence and liberation, 2011

Kuwait's 2023 population was 4.82 million people, of which 1.53 million were Kuwaitis and 3.29 million expatriates.[^cite_note-\:1-19]

### Ethnic groups

[Expatriates in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Expatriates_in_Kuwait) account for around 60% of Kuwait's total population. At the end of December 2018, 57.65% of Kuwait's total population were [Arabs](2185.md) (including Arab expats).[^cite_note-PACI-444] [Indians](7564733.md) and [Egyptians](31912046.md) are the largest expat communities respectively.[^cite_note-445][^cite_note-\:1-19]

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Kuwait)



[Siddiqa Fatima Zahra Mosque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siddiqa_Fatima_Zahra_Mosque) in Kuwait



[Holy Family Co-Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Family_Cathedral,_Kuwait)

Kuwait's official state religion is [Maliki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maliki_school) Sunni Islam. The [Al Sabah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Sabah) ruling family adhere to the Maliki school of Sunni Islam. Most Kuwaiti citizens are Muslim; there is no official national census but it is estimated that 60%–70% are Sunni and 30%–40% are Shia.[^cite_note-446][^cite_note-ssi-447] Kuwait also has a large community of expatriate [Christians](18337522.md), [Hindus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism), [Buddhists](3267529.md), and [Sikhs](27964.md).[^cite_note-irfr2007-448] As of 2020, there are an estimated 837,585 Christians, comprising 17.93% of the population—the second largest religious group.[^cite_note-PACI-444] Most Christians in Kuwait are from [Kerala](4349459.md) in India, namely [Malankara Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malankara_Orthodox_Syrian_Church), [Mar Thoma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mar_Thoma_Syrian_Church), and [Roman Catholic](606848.md). The first Malankara Orthodox parish was St. Thomas Indian Orthodox Pazhayapally Ahmadi, established in 1934.[^cite_note-449] Kuwait includes a [native Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_Kuwait#Kuwaiti_Christians) community, estimated to be composed of between 259 and 400 Kuwaiti citizens.[^cite_note-ref-450] Kuwait is the only [GCC country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cooperation_Council_for_the_Arab_States_of_the_Gulf) besides Bahrain to have a local Christian population who hold citizenship. A small number of Kuwaiti citizens follow the [Baháʼí Faith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith).[^cite_note-irfr2007-448][^cite_note-451]

### Languages

See also: [Kuwaiti Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwaiti_Arabic) and [Kuwaiti Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwaiti_Persian)

Kuwait's official language is [Modern Standard Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Standard_Arabic), but its everyday usage is limited to journalism and education. [Kuwaiti Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwaiti_Arabic) is the variant of Arabic used in everyday life.[^cite_note-452] English is widely understood and often used as a business language. Besides English, French is taught as a third language for the students of the [humanities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humanities) at schools, but for two years only. Kuwaiti Arabic is a variant of [Gulf Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_Arabic), sharing similarities with the dialects of neighboring coastal areas in Eastern Arabia.[^cite_note-453] Due to immigration during its pre-oil history as well as trade, Kuwaiti Arabic borrowed a lot of words from [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language), [Indian languages](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_India), [Balochi language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balochi_language), Turkish, English and Italian.[^cite_note-454]

Due to historical immigration, [Kuwaiti Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwaiti_Persian) is used among [Ajam Kuwaitis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/'Ajam_of_Kuwait).[^cite_note-455][^cite_note-456] The [Iranian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_languages) sub-dialects of [Larestani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Larestani), Khonji, Bastaki and Gerashi also influenced the vocabulary of Kuwaiti Arabic.[^cite_note-457] Most Shia Kuwaiti citizens are of Iranian ancestry.[^cite_note-458][^cite_note-459][^cite_note-460][^cite_note-461][^cite_note-462][^cite_note-463]

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Kuwait)

Kuwait is considered to be a pioneer in the region when it comes to the arts and popular culture.[^cite_note-464][^cite_note-465] Kuwaiti [popular culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_culture), in the form of theatre, radio, music, and television soap opera, flourishes and is even exported to neighboring states.[^cite_note-cliv-466][^cite_note-467] Within the [Arab states of the Persian Gulf](3088555.md), the culture of Kuwait is the closest to the culture of [Bahrain](18933277.md); this is evident in the close association between the two states in theatrical productions and soap operas.[^cite_note-468]

### Performing arts

See also: [Music of Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Kuwait), [Television in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_Kuwait), and [Cinema of Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Kuwait)



A [theatrical play](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Play_\(theatre\)) titled "Fateh Masr" at Al Mubarikya school in the 1940s

Kuwait has the oldest [performing arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Performing_arts) industry in the Arabian Peninsula.[^cite_note-ktv-469] Kuwait's television drama industry is the largest and most active Gulf Arab drama industry and annually produces a minimum of fifteen serials.[^cite_note-omn-470][^cite_note-ahma-471][^cite_note-472] Kuwait is the main production center of the Gulf television drama and comedy scene.[^cite_note-ahma-471] Most Gulf television drama and comedy productions are filmed in Kuwait.[^cite_note-ahma-471][^cite_note-473][^cite_note-474] Kuwaiti soap operas are the most-watched soap operas from the Gulf region.[^cite_note-omn-470][^cite_note-fattah-475][^cite_note-476] Soap operas are most popular during the time of [Ramadan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ramadan), when families gather to break their fast.[^cite_note-477] Although usually performed in the [Kuwaiti dialect](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwaiti_Arabic), they have been shown with success as far away as [Tunisia](30188.md).[^cite_note-478] Kuwait is frequently dubbed the "[Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_Los_Angeles) of the Gulf" due to the popularity of its television soap operas and theatre.[^cite_note-Hollywood-479][^cite_note-480]

Kuwait is the main centre of [scenographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scenography) and [performing arts education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Performing_arts_education) in the GCC region.[^cite_note-481][^cite_note-thae-482] Many famous Middle Eastern actors and singers attribute their success to training in Kuwait.[^cite_note-hub-483] The Higher Institute of Theatrical Arts (HIDA) provides [higher education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Higher_education) in theatrical arts.[^cite_note-thae-482] The institute has several divisions and attracts theatrical students from all over the GCC region. Many actors have graduated from the institute, such as [Souad Abdullah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Souad_Abdullah), Mohammed Khalifa, [Mansour Al-Mansour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mansour_Al-Mansour), along with a number of prominent critics such as [Ismail Fahd Ismail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismail_Fahd_Ismail).

Kuwait is known for its home-grown tradition of [theatre](20913771.md).[^cite_note-hammond-484][^cite_note-485][^cite_note-486] Kuwait is the only country in the Gulf Arab region with a theatrical tradition.[^cite_note-hammond-484] The theatrical movement in Kuwait constitutes a major part of the country's cultural life.[^cite_note-theat-487] Theatrical activities in Kuwait date back to the 1920s when the first spoken dramas were released.[^cite_note-488] Theatre activities are still popular today.[^cite_note-theat-487]

Theatre in Kuwait is [subsidized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subsidized) by the government, previously by the Ministry of Social Affairs and now by the National Council for Culture, Arts, and Letters (NCCAL).[^cite_note-489] Every urban district has a public theatre.[^cite_note-490] The public theatre in [Salmiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salmiya) is named after actor [Abdulhussain Abdulredha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdulhussain_Abdulredha). The annual Kuwait Theater Festival is the largest theatrical arts festival in Kuwait.

Kuwait is the birthplace of various popular musical genres, such as [sawt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sawt_\(music\)) and [fijiri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fijiri).[^cite_note-qdl-491][^cite_note-kwq-492] Traditional Kuwaiti music is a reflection of the country's seafaring heritage,[^cite_note-493] which was influenced by many diverse cultures.[^cite_note-Ya_Bahr-494][^cite_note-495][^cite_note-qdl-491] Kuwait is widely considered the centre of [traditional music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_music) in the GCC region.[^cite_note-qdl-491] Kuwaiti music has considerably influenced the music culture in other GCC countries.[^cite_note-rvo-496][^cite_note-kwq-492] Kuwait pioneered [contemporary Khaliji music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaliji_\(music\)). Kuwaitis were the first commercial recording artists in the Gulf region.[^cite_note-shart-497][^cite_note-pers-498][^cite_note-shartw-499] The first known Kuwaiti recordings were made between 1912 and 1915.[^cite_note-500]

Kuwait is home to various [music festivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_festivals), including the International Music Festival hosted by the National Council for Culture, Arts and Letters (NCCAL).[^cite_note-501][^cite_note-ohiol-502] The [Sheikh Jaber Al-Ahmad Cultural Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Jaber_Al-Ahmad_Cultural_Centre) contains the largest opera house in the Middle East.[^cite_note-503] Kuwait has several academic institutions specializing in university-level [music education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_education).[^cite_note-soton-504][^cite_note-ohiocsw-505] The Higher Institute of Musical Arts was established by the government to provide [bachelor's degrees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelor's_degrees) in music. In addition, the College of Basic Education offers bachelor's degrees in music education. The Institute of Musical Studies offers music education qualifications equivalent to [secondary school](554992.md).[^cite_note-ohiol2-506][^cite_note-ohiocsw-505][^cite_note-soton-504]

Kuwait has a reputation for being the central music influence of the GCC countries.[^cite_note-507] Over the last decade of satellite television stations, many Kuwaiti musicians have become household names in other Arab countries. For example, [Bashar Al Shatty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bashar_Al_Shatty) became famous due to *[Star Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_Academy_\(Arabia\))*. Contemporary Kuwaiti music is popular throughout the Arab world. [Nawal El Kuwaiti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nawal_El_Kuwaiti), Nabeel Shoail and [Abdallah Al Rowaished](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abdallah_Al_Rowaished) are the most popular contemporary performers.[^cite_note-roughguide-508]

### Visual arts



Traditional Kuwaiti wedding dress in the 1970s

Main article: [Art of Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_of_Kuwait)

Kuwait has the oldest [modern arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_arts) movement in the Arabian Peninsula.[^cite_note-movemnt-509][^cite_note-510][^cite_note-511] Beginning in 1936, Kuwait was the first Gulf Arab country to grant scholarships in the arts.[^cite_note-movemnt-509] The Kuwaiti artist [Mojeb al-Dousari](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mojeb_al-Dousari) was the earliest recognized [visual artist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visual_artist) in the Gulf Arab region.[^cite_note-512] He is regarded as the founder of [portrait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portrait) art in the region.[^cite_note-513] The Sultan Gallery was the first professional Arab art gallery in the Gulf.[^cite_note-514][^cite_note-515]

Kuwait is home to more than [30 art galleries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_of_Kuwait#Art_galleries).[^cite_note-516][^cite_note-517] In recent years, Kuwait's contemporary art scene has boomed.[^cite_note-518][^cite_note-519][^cite_note-520] [Khalifa Al-Qattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalifa_Al-Qattan) was the first artist to hold a [solo exhibition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solo_exhibition) in Kuwait. He founded a new art theory in the early 1960s known as "circulism".[^cite_note-521][^cite_note-522] Other notable Kuwaiti artists include [Sami Mohammad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sami_Mohammad), [Thuraya Al-Baqsami](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thuraya_Al-Baqsami) and [Suzan Bushnaq](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suzan_Bushnaq).

The government organizes various [arts festivals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arts_festivals), including the [Al Qurain Cultural Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Qurain_Cultural_Festival) and Formative Arts Festival.[^cite_note-523][^cite_note-524][^cite_note-525] The Kuwait International Biennial was inaugurated in 1967, more than 20 Arab and foreign countries have participated in the biennial.[^cite_note-biennial-526] Prominent participants include [Layla Al-Attar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Layla_Al-Attar). In 2004, the Al Kharafi Biennial for Contemporary Arab Art was inaugurated.

### Cuisine

Main article: [Cuisine of Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cuisine_of_Kuwait)

Kuwaiti cuisine is a fusion of [Arabian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_cuisine), [Iranian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iranian_cuisine), and [Mesopotamian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mesopotamian_cuisine) cuisines. Kuwaiti cuisine is part of the [Eastern Arabian cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Arabian_cuisine). A prominent dish in Kuwaiti cuisine is *[machboos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Machboos)*, a rice-based dish usually prepared with [basmati](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basmati) rice seasoned with spices, and chicken or mutton.

[Seafood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seafood) is a significant part of the Kuwaiti diet, especially [fish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fish).[^cite_note-527] [Mutabbaq samak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mutabbaq_samak) is a national dish in Kuwait. Other local favourites are *hamour* ([grouper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grouper)), which is typically served grilled, fried, or with [biryani](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Biryani) rice because of its texture and taste; *safi* ([rabbitfish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rabbitfish)); *maid* ([mulletfish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulletfish)); and *sobaity* ([sea bream](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_bream)).

Kuwait's traditional [flatbread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flatbread) is called Iranian *khubz*. It is a large flatbread baked in a special oven and it is often topped with sesame seeds. Numerous local bakeries dot the country; the bakers are mainly Iranians (hence the name of the bread, "Iranian *khubuz*").

### Museums

Main article: [List of museums in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Kuwait)



[Sheikh Jaber Al-Ahmad Cultural Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Jaber_Al-Ahmad_Cultural_Centre)



The [Al-Hashemi-II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Hashemi-II), the largest wooden dhow ever built

The new [Kuwait National Cultural District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_National_Cultural_District) (KNCD) consists of various cultural venues including [Sheikh Abdullah Al Salem Cultural Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Abdullah_Al_Salem_Cultural_Centre), [Sheikh Jaber Al Ahmad Cultural Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheikh_Jaber_Al_Ahmad_Cultural_Centre), [Al Shaheed Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Shaheed_Park), and [Al Salam Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Salam_Palace_\(Kuwait\)).[^cite_note-kncd-412][^cite_note-kncd2-411] With a capital cost of more than US\$1 billion, it is one of the largest cultural districts in the world.[^cite_note-kncd-412] The Abdullah Salem Cultural Centre is the largest museum complex in the Middle East.[^cite_note-528][^cite_note-auto3-529] The Kuwait National Cultural District is a member of the [Global Cultural Districts Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Cultural_Districts_Network).[^cite_note-gcdn-415]

[Sadu House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sadu_House) is among Kuwait's most important cultural institutions. [Bait Al-Othman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bait_Al-Othman_Museum) is the largest museum specializing in Kuwait's history. [The Scientific Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Scientific_Center) is one of the largest science museums in the Middle East. The [Museum of Modern Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Modern_Art_\(Kuwait\)) showcases the history of modern art in Kuwait and the region.[^cite_note-530] The Kuwait Maritime Museum presents the country's maritime heritage in the pre-oil era. Several traditional Kuwaiti dhow ships are open to the public, such as [Fateh Al-Khayr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fateh_Al-Khayr) and [Al-Hashemi-II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Hashemi-II) which entered the [Guinness Book of World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_Book_of_World_Records) as the largest wooden dhow ever built.[^cite_note-tookuw-531][^cite_note-532] The [Historical, Vintage, and Classical Cars Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical,_Vintage,_and_Classical_Cars_Museum) displays vintage cars from Kuwait's motoring heritage. The [National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_National_Museum), established in 1983, has been described as "underused and overlooked".[^cite_note-\:5-112]

Several Kuwaiti museums are devoted to [Islamic art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islamic_art), most notably the [Tareq Rajab Museums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tareq_Rajab_Museum) and [Dar al Athar al Islamiyyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dar_al_Athar_al_Islamiyyah) cultural centres.[^cite_note-tareqrajabmuseum-533][^cite_note-modmus-534][^cite_note-535][^cite_note-dai-536] The Dar al Athar al Islamiyyah cultural centres include education wings, conservation labs, and research libraries.[^cite_note-dai-536][^cite_note-dai_two-537] There are several [art libraries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Libraries) in Kuwait.[^cite_note-538][^cite_note-dai-536][^cite_note-539][^cite_note-dai_two-537] [Khalifa Al-Qattan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khalifa_Al-Qattan)'s Mirror House is the most popular art museum in Kuwait.[^cite_note-540] Many museums in Kuwait are private enterprises.[^cite_note-trans-541][^cite_note-modmus-534] In contrast to the top-down approach in other Gulf states, museum development in Kuwait reflects a greater sense of civic identity and demonstrates the strength of civil society in Kuwait, which has produced many independent cultural enterprises.[^cite_note-542][^cite_note-modmus-534][^cite_note-trans-541]

### Society

Urban Kuwaiti society is more [open](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_society) than other Gulf Arab societies.[^cite_note-543] Kuwaiti citizens are ethnically diverse, consisting of both Arabs and [Persians (Ajam)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ajam_of_Kuwait).[^cite_note-venez2-544][^cite_note-545][^cite_note-546] Kuwait stands out in the region as the most liberal in empowering women in the public sphere.[^cite_note-547][^cite_note-548][^cite_note-549] [Kuwaiti women](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women_in_Kuwait) outnumber men in the workforce.[^cite_note-gnw-276] Kuwaiti political scientist Ghanim Alnajjar sees these qualities as a manifestation of Kuwaiti society as a whole, whereby in the Gulf Arab region it is "the least strict about traditions".[^cite_note-\:6-279]<sup>: 190 </sup>

### Media



The 372 m (1,220 ft) tall [Liberation Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Tower_\(Kuwait\)) (left) is the main communication tower of Kuwait.

Kuwait produces more [newspapers and magazines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_newspapers_in_Kuwait) per capita than its neighbors.[^cite_note-550][^cite_note-551] The state-owned [Kuwait News Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_News_Agency) (KUNA) is the largest media house in the country. The Ministry of Information regulates the media industry in Kuwait. Kuwait's media is annually classified as partly free in the [Freedom of Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_Press) survey by Freedom House.[^cite_note-552] Since 2005,[^cite_note-prfr-553] Kuwait has frequently earned the highest ranking of all Arab countries in the annual [Press Freedom Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Press_Freedom_Index) by Reporters Without Borders.[^cite_note-fanak-554][^cite_note-isr-555][^cite_note-indi-556][^cite_note-indextwo-557][^cite_note-558][^cite_note-559][^cite_note-560][^cite_note-ofa-561][^cite_note-562] In 2009, 2011, 2013 and 2014, Kuwait surpassed Israel as the country with the greatest press freedom in the Middle East.[^cite_note-fanak-554][^cite_note-isr-555][^cite_note-indi-556][^cite_note-indextwo-557][^cite_note-ofa-561] Kuwait is also frequently ranked as the Arab country with the greatest press freedom in Freedom House's annual Freedom of Press survey.[^cite_note-lead-563][^cite_note-564][^cite_note-565][^cite_note-566][^cite_note-567][^cite_note-568][^cite_note-569]

Kuwait has 15 satellite television channels, of which four are controlled by the Ministry of Information. State-owned [Kuwait Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Television) (KTV) offered first colored broadcast in 1974 and operated five television channels. Government-funded Radio Kuwait also offers daily informative programming in several languages including [Arabic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabic_language), [Persian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_language), [Urdu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urdu), and English on the [AM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AM_broadcasting) and [SW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shortwave).

### Literature

Main article: [Kuwaiti literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwaiti_literature)

Kuwait has in recent years produced several prominent contemporary writers such as [Ismail Fahd Ismail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ismail_Fahd_Ismail), author of over twenty novels and numerous short story collections. There is also evidence that Kuwaiti literature has long been interactive with [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_literature) and [French literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/French_literature).[^cite_note-570]

### Sport



Kuwaiti women at a local football match

Main article: [Football in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Kuwait)

[Football](10568.md) is the most popular sport in Kuwait. The [Kuwait Football Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Football_Association) (KFA) is the governing body of football in Kuwait. The KFA organizes the [men's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_national_football_team), [women's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_women's_national_football_team), and [futsal](188197.md) national teams. The Kuwait national football team have been the champions of the Asian Cup in [1980](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_AFC_Asian_Cup), runners-up in [1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_AFC_Asian_Cup), and have taken third place in [1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_AFC_Asian_Cup). Kuwait has also been to one FIFA World Cup, in [1982](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_FIFA_World_Cup); they drew 1–1 with [Czechoslovakia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czechoslovakia_national_football_team) before losing to [France](250197.md) and [England](9904.md), failing to advance from the first round. Kuwait has also won the most [Gulf Cups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arabian_Gulf_Cup), with them being crowned champions 10 times in [1970](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations), [1972](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations), [1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations), [1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations), [1982](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations), [1986](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations), [1990](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1990_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations), [1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1996_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations), [1998](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1998_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations) and [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Gulf_Cup_of_Nations). The [Kuwaiti Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwaiti_Premier_League) is the top league of Kuwaiti football. Kuwait is home to many football clubs including [Al-Arabi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Arabi_SC_\(Kuwait\)), [Burgan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burgan_SC), [Fahaheel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Fahaheel_SC), [Al-Jahra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Jahra_\(football_club\)), [Al-Jazeera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Jazeera_FC), [Kazma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Kazma_Kuwait), [Khaitan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khaitan_Sporting_Club), [Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_SC), [Al-Nasr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Nasr_SC_\(Kuwait\)), [Al-Sahel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sahel_\(Kuwaiti_football_club\)), [Al-Salmiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Salmiya_Club), [Al-Shabab](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shabab_\(Kuwait\)), [Al-Shamiya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al-Shamiya_FC), [Sporty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sporty_FC), [Sulaibikhat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulaibikhat_SC), [Qadsia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qadsia_SC) [Al-Tadhamon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Tadhamon_SC) and [Yarmouk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Al_Yarmouk_\(football_club\)). The biggest football rivalry in Kuwait is between [Al-Arabi and Qadsia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwaiti_El_Cl%C3%A1sico).

[Basketball](3921.md) is one of the country's most popular sports. The [Kuwait national basketball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_national_basketball_team) is governed by the Kuwait Basketball Association (KBA). Kuwait made its international debut in 1959. The national team has been to the [FIBA Asian Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIBA_Asian_Championship) in basketball eleven times. The [Kuwaiti Division I Basketball League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwaiti_Division_I_Basketball_League) is the highest professional basketball league in Kuwait. [Cricket in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_national_cricket_team) is governed by the [Kuwait Cricket Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Cricket_Association). Other growing sports include [rugby union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rugby_union_in_Kuwait). [Handball](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_men's_national_handball_team) is widely considered to be the national icon of Kuwait, although football is more popular among the overall population.



[Jaber Al-Ahmad International Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jaber_Al-Ahmad_International_Stadium)

[Ice hockey in Kuwait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_national_ice_hockey_team) is governed by the [Kuwait Ice Hockey Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kuwait_Ice_Hockey_Association). Kuwait first joined the [International Ice Hockey Federation](16760449.md) in 1985, but was expelled in 1992 due to a lack of ice hockey activity.[^cite_note-571] Kuwait was re-admitted into the IIHF in May 2009.[^cite_note-572] In 2015, Kuwait won the [IIHF Challenge Cup of Asia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IIHF_Challenge_Cup_of_Asia).[^cite_note-573][^cite_note-574]

In February 2020, Kuwait held for the first time a leg of the [UIM Aquabike World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aquabike_World_Championship_\(powerboating\)) in front of Marina Beach City.[^cite_note-575]

In May 2022, Kuwait hosted the Third Gulf Cooperation Council (GCC) Games at the 360 Marina. The event featured 16 different sports, including volleyball, basketball, swimming, athletics, karate and judo and attracted over 1,700 male and female players.[^cite_note-576]
